      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3300313" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The first step in wound management should be an </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">assessment of the overall stability</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> of the animal.       downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Pearls & Pitfalls</span></p></h3></div><div tabindex="-1" class="TopicTableView_tableWrap__ZzPqw    "><table class="TopicTableView_tableViewWrapper__NC6RG "><tbody class="undefined"><tr data-testid="TopicTableBodyView"><td data-testid="TopicTableCellView" rowspan="" colSpan="" class="TopicTableCellView_tableData__pQ2zv TopicTableCellView_notinpopup__lcznU "><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            TopicList_tableListView__w4iky undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375269" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Obvious open wounds can distract attention from more subtle but potentially life-threatening problems (eg, shock). </span></p></li></ul></td></tr></tbody></table><div class="TopicTableView_mediaprintfooter__cNGyB false"></div></div></div></div></div></div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375328" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">After initial assessment, the patient should be stabilized. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375329" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">First aid</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> for the wound should be performed as soon as safely possible. Active bleeding can be controlled with direct pressure. A pneumatic cuff or pressure wrap, instead of a tourniquet, should be used in cases of severe arterial bleeding; the cuff should be inflated until hemorrhage is controlled. Use of a cuff or pressure wrap helps avoid neurovascular complications that can be associated with narrow tourniquets.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3300314" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Treatment for any local wound should be </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">guided by the fundamentals of debridement, infection or inflammation control, and moisture balance</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">. The wound must be protected from further contamination or trauma by covering it with a sterile, lint-free dressing. The delay between examination and definitive debridement should be minimized to decrease bacterial contamination. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375330" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">If the wound is infected, a sample should be collected for bacteriologic culture and antimicrobial susceptibility testing. Antimicrobial treatment should be instituted in all cases of dirty, infected, or puncture wounds. A broad-spectrum bactericidal antimicrobial (eg, a first-generation cephalosporin) is generally recommended pending culture results. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375331" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Analgesia is indicated for pain relief.</span></p><span id="wound-lavage" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3300315" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3300316" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="Wound-Lavage_v3300315" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Wound Lavage" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Wound Lavage in Small Animals</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3300318" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Wound lavage serves two purposes. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375333" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Irrigation of the wound washes away both visible and microscopic debris. This </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">decreases the bacterial load</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> in the tissue, which helps decrease wound complications. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375334" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The lavage also </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">allows better examination of underlying tissues</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375335" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Assuming the solution is nontoxic, the most important factor in wound lavage is use of large volumes to facilitate removal of debris. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375336" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Wound lavage is </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">most effective when delivered under appropriate pressure</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">. Low-pressure systems, such as a bulb syringe, are adequate in clean wounds. Higher pressures are used in dirty or contaminated wounds. A recommended lavage system delivering 48–55 kPa (7–8 psi) of pressure can be fashioned with a 35-mL syringe and a 19-gauge needle. Excessive pressure can cause adverse effects by driving debris deeper into the healthy tissue. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375337" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The use of antimicrobials in the lavage fluid is controversial.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3300319" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The ideal lavage fluid should be antiseptic and nontoxic to the healing tissues. Saline (0.9% NaCl) solution is the least toxic to healing tissue, although it is not antiseptic. Surgical scrub agents should not be used because the detergent components are damaging to tissue. Dilute antiseptics can be used safely. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375338" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Chlorhexidine diacetate (0.05%) has sustained residual activity against a broad spectrum of bacteria while causing minimal tissue inflammation. However, gram-negative bacteria may become resistant to chlorhexidine. Stronger solutions of chlorhexidine are toxic to healing tissue. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375339" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Povidone-iodine is an effective antiseptic; however, it has minimal residual activity and may be inactivated by purulent debris. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375340" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Although an effective </span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/pharmacology/antiseptics-and-disinfectants" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">antiseptic</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">, hydrogen peroxide is toxic to healthy tissue and should not be used for lavage of wounds.</span></p></div></section><span id="surgical-debridement" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3300320" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3300321" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="Surgical-Debridement_v3300320" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Surgical Debridement" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Surgical Debridement in Small Animals</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3300324" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">After wound preparation and hair removal, surgical debridement (removal of nonviable tissue and foreign debris) can be performed. Debridement may involve nonselective removal of large segments of tissue or may be performed more selectively, enabling preservation of specific tissues. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375343" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Skin and local tissue viability should be assessed before any attempted debridement. Skin that is blue-black, leathery, thin, or white is usually not viable. This necrotic tissue should be sharply excised. The debridement may be done in layers or as one complete section of tissue (en bloc resection). </span></p><div class="false" data-testid="topicImage"><div class="TopicImage_ariaAnnouncement__Q7eDv" aria-live="polite"></div><div tabindex="0" dir="ltr" class="TopicImage_topicImageComponentSideline__iDQ4J veterinary undefined" aria-expanded="false" role="button" aria-label="Thermal burn, dog Expand the Image"><div class="TopicImage_topicImageContainer__xGEDy TopicImage_topicImageContainerSideline__Gae5m  printBreakInside innerCurvedBorder" id="v14479039"><div class="TopicImage_topicImageHeader___E3ey"><span class="TopicImage_topicImageTitle__0Y8Op false">Thermal burn, dog</span><div data-testid="expandButton" class="ExpandButtonComponent_veterinary__YQfRs ExpandButtonComponent_expandIcon__hjSiD false

Winkler.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375344" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Tissues that have questionable viability or are associated with essential structures such as neurovascular bundles should be treated conservatively. Staged debridement is indicated in some situations. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375345" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">In addition to sharp dissection, debridement may be autolytic, mechanical (eg, bandage debridement), enzymatic, or biological (maggot therapy).</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375346" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Selective trimming of viable tissue at wound edges (ie, "freshening the edges") is generally not indicated in acute wound management, although it may facilitate wound closure when the shape of the wound makes closure difficult.</span></p></div></section><span id="wound-closure" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3300326" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3300327" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="Wound-Closure_v3300326" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Wound Closure" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Wound Closure in Small Animals</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3300329" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">After initial inspection, lavage, and debridement, a veterinarian must decide whether to close the wound or to manage it as an open wound. Considerations include the availability of skin for closure, wound location, and level of contamination or infection. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375348" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Although </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">primary closure</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> is the simplest method of wound management, it should be used only in appropriate situations to avoid wound complications. </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Wounds may be closed with suture, staples, or cyanoacrylate. </span></b></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375349" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Clean wounds that are properly debrided usually heal without complication. With a primary closure, the layers should be individually closed to minimize “dead space” that might contribute to seroma formation. The types of suture and suture patterns used depend on surgeon preference, the size of the wound, the location of the wound, and the size of the animal.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3300330" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Primary closure may not be appropriate for a grossly contaminated or infected wound. Therefore, if closure is the goal, it may be delayed until the contamination or infection is controlled. The wound can be managed short-term as an open wound until it appears healthy. At that time, the wound can be safely closed with minimal risk of complications. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375350" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The time between initial debridement and final closure varies according to the extent of contamination or infection. Minimally contaminated wounds may be closed after 24–72 hours. Longer periods may be required for heavily infected wounds.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3300331" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Wounds closed > 5 days after the initial wounding are considered to be </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">secondary closure</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">. This implies that granulation tissue has begun to form in the wound before closure.</span></p></div></section><span id="open-wound-management" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3300332" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3300333" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="Open-Wound-Management_v3300332" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Open Wound Management" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Open Wound Management in Small Animals</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3300335" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">When a wound cannot or should not be closed, open wound management </span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">(ie, second-intention healing) is appropriate. Such wounds include those in which there has been a </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">loss of skin that makes closure impossible and those that are too grossly infected to close</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">. Longitudinal degloving injuries of the extremities are especially amenable to open wound management. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375352" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">If the wound is left open, it should be managed for optimal healing.</span></p><div class="false" data-testid="topicImage"><div class="TopicImage_ariaAnnouncement__Q7eDv" aria-live="polite"></div><div tabindex="0" dir="ltr" class="TopicImage_topicImageComponentSideline__iDQ4J veterinary undefined" aria-expanded="false" role="button" aria-label="Wound management decision tree Expand the Image"><div class="TopicImage_topicImageContainer__xGEDy TopicImage_topicImageContainerSideline__Gae5m  printBreakInside innerCurvedBorder" id="v82375272"><div class="TopicImage_topicImageHeader___E3ey"><span class="TopicImage_topicImageTitle__0Y8Op false">Wound management decision tree</span><div data-testid="expandButton" class="ExpandButtonComponent_veterinary__YQfRs ExpandButtonComponent_expandIcon__hjSiD false

loading="lazy"/></noscript></span></div><div class="TopicThumbnail_tag__WXR4w undefined">Image</div></div></div></div></div><div class="TopicImage_mediaPrintDescription__giskr TopicImage_displaynone__4Pxtz false" tabindex="-1"><span id="v113849616" class="anchor"></span><div class="para"> <p>Progressive wound contracture resulting in loss of range of motion in the shoulder.</p></div></div><div class="TopicImage_description__n9qkZ false" tabindex="-1"><span id="v44176133" class="anchor" ></span><div class="para" > <p >Courtesy of Dr. Kevin Winkler.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375353" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Open wound management enables progressive debridement procedures and does not require specialized equipment (such as may be needed with skin grafting). However, it increases cost, prolongs time for healing, and may create complications from wound contracture.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3300336" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Open wound management is based on </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">repeated bandaging and debridement as needed</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> until the wound heals. Traditional open wound management calls for </span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/emergency-medicine-and-critical-care/wound-management-in-small-animals/wound-bandages-and-dressings-for-small-animals" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">wet adherent (wet-to-dry) dressings</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> initially. The initial wide-mesh gauze dressings help with mechanical debridement at every bandage change. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375355" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Until a granulation bed forms, the bandage should be changed at least once daily. In the early stages of healing, the bandage may need to be changed as often as twice daily. After granulation tissue develops, the bandage should be changed to a dry, nonadherent dressing so the granulation bed is not disrupted. Both the granulation bed and the early epithelium are easily damaged, and disruption of the granulation bed delays wound healing.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3300337" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">With the concept of moist wound healing, bandaging is combined with autolytic debridement to promote wound healing. The use of moist wound dressings keeps white cells healthier, allowing them to aid in the debridement process. A variety of dressings are available. With these newer dressing options, some consider traditional wet-to-dry dressings to be outdated.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v44176181" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">A decision to use open wound management or delayed wound closure is based on several factors: </span></p><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375358" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">patient morbidity</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375360" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">cost</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375362" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">wound location</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375364" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">technical expertise required for closure</span></p></li></ul><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375366" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">In certain patients, surgery may not be an option given their health status. For those patients, open wound management is a better choice. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375367" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Although the initial cost of surgery is higher than bandage changes, the overall cost may be lower, depending on the nature and length of wound care required without surgical intervention. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375368" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Wound location also has a major impact on the decision to surgically intervene. As the open wound heals, contracture of the wound begins to occur. Contracture of a wound on the lateral thorax may not result in any longterm complications. However, a wound spanning or adjacent to a joint may result in loss of longterm joint function or range of motion due to scarring. To avoid this loss of function, the wound should be surgically closed. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v44176184" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Another component of decision-making in these cases relates to the clinician's abilities. If the techniques required for closure exceed the clinician's expertise, referral to another location for closure is advisable to avoid unnecessary complications. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375369" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Finally, the owner may have a specific request regarding cosmesis. Closure with open wound management will result in a hairless scar. For various reasons, this may not be acceptable. In those cases, surgical intervention is required. </span></p></div></section><span id="key-points" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v82375370" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v82375371" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="Key-Points_v82375370" class="TopicFHead_showKeyPoints__RB4kR veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 id="KeyPoints_v82375371" class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Key Points" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Key Points</span><span class="TopicFHead_keypoints__9WCzn"></span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375374" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Stabilizing injured patients should take priority over wound management.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375376" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Wounds should be irrigated (lavage), with an appropriate solution at appropriate pressure, to remove contamination (bacteria and debris) and improve visibility for wound inspection.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375378" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Surgical debridement removes nonviable tissue.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v82375380" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Options for wound management are closure (which may be primary or secondary) and open wound management (second-intention healing).</span></p></li></ul></div></section><span id="for-more-information" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v44176275" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v44176276" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="For-More-Information_v44176275" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 id="Further_v44176276" class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
